No threshold seed or full-cone sufficiency.", "evidence": "# Complete calibration comparison on the lower-first-step domain\n\nStatus: complete joint mathematical DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. This is max4\nround36, the second and last analytical round selected by TASK.md and\nmaster_guidance4226374d0649bf55 after completed baseline34. No numerical\nevaluation, solver, machine algebra, replay, formal verifier, consult,\ninstallation or subagent is used. Accepted mathematical inputs are\nfacts04219554e41d9750, Section10, and1252b8823bb5a290. All additional\narguments needed here are proved in this document, including the full\noriginal-array necessity arguments reproduced in Appendix A.\n\nThis closes the previously unresolved comparison of the calibrated\nfamilies when the first step is below sqrt(2) and D<0. It also closes\nevery mixing weight on the whole lower-first-step, upper-terminal\ndomain. It does not construct a threshold seed. No statement in this\ndocument is promoted to the fact graph.\n\n## 1. Definitions and exact comparison statements\n\nUse local parameters\n\n    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,v), a,b,c>0, bc<=1, 0<v<2,\n    u=1+a, beta=1+b, w=1+c,\n    A=2+a, N=3+a+b, C=1+sum h=4+a+b+c+v,\n    t=c/(1+v), mu=1+t, gamma=w/mu,\n    ell=a^2*b*(1-b*t),\n    R=ell*N^2+mu-C^2*a^2*b*beta*t^2,\n    D=b^2*N^2-A^2,\n    Q=1-C^2*a^2*b^2*c^2*(v-1)^2,\n    U=A^2-C^2*b^2*c^2*(v-1)^2, V=Q/a^2,\n    delta=u^2-2, Delta=1/a^2-A^2=-u^2*delta/a^2.\n\nThe letter V here is a local scalar, not the primitive energy V_h.\nThe constants H,k,k0 are\n\n H=(2*b*w-beta\n     +sqrt((beta-2*b*w)^2+4*b*w^2*(1-b*t)/mu))/(2*(1-b*t)),\n k=H/(a^2*mu), k0=b^2*w/(ell*mu), eps=k*R-D.\n\nThe root H is positive; Appendix A proves k>k0>0. Put\n\n    eta=b/(1+w+v),\n    F2=A^2-C^2*eta^2, F3=N^2-C^2*t^2,\n    nu=(1+eta)*(1-a*eta)/a,\n    Erow=a+3+1/a-(u*delta/a)*eta-C^2*eta^2,\n    Qplus=Q+u^2*delta=a^2*U,\n    S=sqrt(Qplus)-sqrt(Q).\n\nSquare roots of Q and Qplus are used only when both are nonnegative.\nThe quantities F2 and F3 are the OLD scalar second- and third-cut\nenergies. The quantities Q,U,R are the OLD unit-quadratic, scalar-return\nand repaired-third-cut energies. Their complete legal witnesses are\nproved below. The old terminal cut is\n\n                         v<=2C/(C+1).                  (1)\n\nAppendix A defines the actual original-row coefficient sigma>0, its\nsign caps, and the terminal function Psi. The entire calibrated family\nhas the form\n\n eps+lambda*Erow\n     >= r*u*delta-r^2*Q/(sigma+lambda*nu-r*u),          (2)\n\nfor every lambda>=0 and every price r satisfying its original incoming\ncoefficient caps and r<(sigma+lambda*nu)/u. The case lambda=0 is the\nunmixed family. A mixing weight lambda is distinct from every original\nmultiplier Lambda_ij. No original coefficient is independently selected\nin deriving (2). Every mixed-family conclusion below is on a domain\nwhere nu>0, so sigma+lambda*nu is positive for all lambda>=0.\n\nHere are the complete comparison conclusions.\n\nI. On the concrete variable-first-step domain\n\n    0<a<=sqrt(2)-1, D<=0, b,c>0, bc<=1, 0<v<2,        (3)\n\nthe OLD conditions F2>=0,F3>=0,Q>=0,U>=0 and (1) imply R>=0,\neps>=0, EVERY original price/cap branch in (2) for EVERY lambda>=0,\nand the ENTIRE terminal condition R<=Psi(sqrt(eps)). This comparison\nincludes terminal steps below sqrt(2), and requires neither ab<=1 nor\nc*(v-1)<=1. For a<sqrt(2)-1 the remainder eps is strictly positive.\n\nII. On the whole upper-terminal domain\n\n    a,b,c>0, bc<=1, sqrt(2)<=v<2,                     (4)\n\nthe OLD conditions R>=0,Q>=0,U>=0,F3>=0 and (1) imply eps>=0,\nEVERY unmixed calibration price/cap branch, and the ENTIRE terminal\ncondition. There is now NO remaining first-step or D-sign exception\nfor the unmixed family on (4).\n\nOn (4) with 0<a<=sqrt(2)-1, adding only the OLD second cut F2>=0\nalso implies EVERY lambda>=0 mixture in (2). Thus the whole calibrated\nsubsystem is redundant on the entire lower-first-step, upper-terminal\ndomain, including all a<sqrt(2)-1,D<0. The capacity is unrestricted,\nso these implications hold on intersection with C>=193/25 as well.\n\nIII. For comparison with the higher-first-step regime, on (4) with\n\n sqrt(2)-1<=a<1, ab<=1, c*(v-1)<=1, a*eta<1,\n\nretain the FULL older first-row condition\n\n Erow>=0,\n u*delta<=u*Erow/nu+2*sqrt(Q*Erow/nu).                (5)\n\nThen every lambda>=0 mixture is again implied. This last statement\nis rederived to give a joint comparison; it is not a new exclusion.\n\nIn I--III, intersect with EVERY other older cut, clipping-return,\nadjacent/lifted triangle, C82/C84, C83/C86 curvature, endpoint and\nfirst-row condition. The implication is preserved because a specified\nSUBSET of the complete older conjunction already suffices. No old\ncondition is deleted to construct a survivor, and no nonemptiness of\nthe complete conjunction is claimed. These are necessity comparisons,\nnot sufficiency results for the full original cone.\n\n## 2. Original interface and the older inputs\n\nFor indices0 through4 keep all twenty nonnegative ordered multipliers\nLambda_ij, with Lambda_ii=0. Let e be the all-ones column, define\n\n T_ii=1, T_ij=h_j for j<i, T_ij=0 for j>i,\n L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda,\n omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2sum h)^T,\n D_prim=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)),\n s=C-1, M=D_prim+(L_flow*T+T^T*L_flow^T)/2.\n\nThe full sufficient seed condition is L_flow^T e=omega and M>=0\non ALL of R^5. No support, symmetry, rank, reversal or gradient\nidentification is imposed. Fact04219554e41d9750 proves the exact\nidentity on formal data obeying x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i:\n\n V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=sum_(i,j)M_ij<g_i,g_j>,\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                             -s(s+1)||g4||^2. (6)\n\nEvery actual smooth convex trajectory has Q_ij<=0 by accepted\n1252b8823bb5a290. Thus a legal trajectory with V_h<0 excludes the\noriginal full condition. For saturated values\nf_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, telescoping the four updates gives\n\n    V_h=||x0||^2-||x4+s*g4||^2,\n    Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j>.                           (7)\n\nFor the scalar second cut use the function with derivative\nclamp(x,[-eta,1]), with value0 at0, started at A. Its trajectory is\n\n x=(A,1,-b,-(1+v)*eta,-eta),\n g=(1,1,-eta,-eta,-eta).\n\nThe relation b=(1+w+v)*eta checks the last two updates. All points\nfrom index2 on lie at or below -eta, so every displayed gradient is\ncorrect. The values are saturated and (7) gives exactly F2.\n\nFor the scalar third cut use derivative clamp(x,[-t,1]), started at N:\n\n x=(N,2+b,1,-c,-t), g=(1,1,1,-t,-t).\n\nHere c=(1+v)*t, so the final update is correct, and (7) gives F3.\nBoth derivatives are continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz. Their\nintegrals are convex differentiable1-smooth functions with minimizer0.\nThis proves F2>=0 and F3>=0 as old necessary conditions.\n\nFor Q take f(x)=x^2/2, started at1. Its gradient column is\n(1,-a,ab,-abc,abc*(v-1)); x4=g4, and (7) gives Q.\n\nFor U take f'(x)=min(x,1), with f(0)=0, started at A. Its iterates\nand gradients are\n\n x=(A,1,-b,bc,bc*(1-v)),\n g=(1,1,-b,bc,bc*(1-v)).\n\nThe assumption bc<=1 makes the index3 gradient correct. If v>=1,\nthe last point is nonpositive; if 0<v<1, it lies in[0,bc]. Thus the\nlast gradient is correct on the ENTIRE interval0<v<2. The function\nis convex differentiable1-smooth with unique minimizer0. Formula(7)\ngives U, so Q>=0,U>=0 are old inputs throughout the stated domains.\n\nFor R, combine two scalar arrays orthogonally with squared weights\nell and mu:\n\n x_base=(N,2+b,1,-c,-t), g_base=(1,1,1,-t,-t),\n x_aux=(1,-a,ab,-abc,-abt), g_aux=(1,-a,ab,-abt,-abt).\n\nBoth arrays obey the updates. Give the combined samples saturated\nvalues and add the zero optimum. The complete nonzero finite residuals\nare\n\n Q30=Q40=-ell*mu*(2+a+b),\n Q31=Q41=-ell*mu*beta,\n Q03=-a*b*u*mu*v*t,\n Q23=-a^2*b*beta*mu*v*t.                              (8)\n\nEvery other ordered residual is zero. For an explicit check use the\nbase normals (2+a+b,beta,0,-v*t,0) and auxiliary normals\n(0,0,0,-ab*v*t,0). The potentially positive Q13 terms cancel by\nell=a^2*b*(1-bt). The optimum residuals have one orientation zero;\nthe opposite ones are negatives of <g_i,x_i-g_i>, which are\nnonnegative before negation. At index3 this inner product is\nell*v*t^2+mu*a^2*b^2*v*t^2. At indices0 and1 it is the nonnegative\nbase contribution, and at2 and4 it is zero. Accepted1252b8823bb5a290\ntherefore realizes these full jets by one static function. Their energy\nis R, since ell+mu*a^2*b^2=a^2*b*beta. This proves R>=0.\n\nFor (1), when v>1 use derivative clamp(x,[-(v-1),1]), started at\nC-v=1+h0+h1+h2. Its gradients are1 through index3 and -(v-1) at4,\nwhere x4=g4. Its energy is (C-v)^2-C^2*(v-1)^2. Factoring the\npositive sum factor gives (1). At0<v<=1, inequality(1) is automatic\nbecause C>1 and 2C/(C+1)>1. Appendix A also obtains (1) from the\noriginal terminal row, without any witness restriction.\n\n## 3. The decisive old-cut identities\n\nDirectly regroup the definition of R:\n\n R=mu*(1-a^2*b^2*N^2)+a^2*b*beta*F3\n  =a^2*mu*(Delta-D)+a^2*b*beta*F3.\n\nHere beta-b*mu=1-b*t and Delta-D=1/a^2-b^2*N^2. Multiplying by\nk=H/(a^2*mu) and subtracting D proves the EXACT identity\n\n eps=H*Delta-(H+1)*D+(H*b*beta/mu)*F3.                (9)\n\nIt is an identity in the steps, before choosing any original array.\nOn domain(3), Delta>=0 and D<=0. Hence F3>=0 implies\n\n                 R>=0, eps>=H*Delta>=0.               (10)\n\nIf a<sqrt(2)-1, then Delta>0, making the last bound strictly positive.\nNo lower bound on Delta,D,F3 or their nonzero margins is assumed.\n\nThe second old cut gives another EXACT identity:\n\n            Erow+nu*delta=F2-delta*(1+eta)^2.          (11)\n\nExpand nu=(1+(1-a)*eta-a*eta^2)/a and use\nA^2-delta=2a+5 to verify the constant, linear and quadratic\ncoefficients. If a<=sqrt(2)-1 and F2>=0, then eta<=A/C<1.\nSince a<1, this ensures nu>0. In this domain delta<=0, and (11)\ntherefore gives\n\n                       Erow>=-nu*delta>=0.             (12)\n\nThe original row proof of the meaning of Erow is in Appendix A.\nThis comparison does not assume that one can choose its Gram entries\nindependently of eps.\n\n## 4. All calibration prices and all mixing weights on domain(3)\n\nAppendix A proves, with its actual original correction qcorr>=0,\n\n 0<sigma=H*u^2/a^2-(H*u/a+qcorr/beta)^2/(1+H)\n             <=sigma0:=H*u^2/(a^2*(1+H)).             (13)\n\nFor every S0>0, Q>=0 and Qplus>=0, the supremum over ALL real\nr<S0/u of\n\n             r*u*delta-r^2*Q/(S0-r*u)\n\nis exactly S0*S^2/u^2. To prove it set y=S0-r*u>0. The expression\nbecomes\n\n S0*delta+2*S0*Q/u^2-Qplus*y/u^2-Q*S0^2/(u^2*y).\n\nThe square of sqrt(Qplus*y)-S0*sqrt(Q/y) is nonnegative, giving\nthe upper bound. Equality is attained when both energies are positive;\nwhen exactly one is zero, let y tend to0 or infinity as appropriate.\nIf both energies are zero, delta=0 and the expression is identically0.\nThus this is a supremum statement including all zero-energy limits.\nImposing any additional original sign cap on r cannot raise it.\n\nOn a<=sqrt(2)-1, one has 0<=Qplus<=Q. Consequently\n\n S^2=(sqrt(Q)-sqrt(Qplus))^2<=Q-Qplus=-u^2*delta.       (14)\n\nEquations(10),(13),(14) now prove\n\n sigma0*S^2/u^2<=-sigma0*delta\n       =H*Delta/(1+H)<=eps.                           (15)\n\nThe older scalar cut, not a newly assumed row budget, supplies the\nlast inequality. Likewise (12),(14) give\n\n                      Erow>=nu*S^2/u^2.               (16)\n\nFor EVERY lambda>=0, add (15) and lambda times (16):\n\n eps+lambda*Erow\n       >=(sigma0+lambda*nu)*S^2/u^2\n       >=(sigma+lambda*nu)*S^2/u^2.                   (17)\n\nApply the unrestricted supremum with S0=sigma+lambda*nu. This proves\nEVERY member of (2), including all cap branches. No selected price,\nselected lambda, asymptotic price limit or positive-energy branch is\nsubstituted for the whole family.\n\n## 5. The terminal test on domain(3)\n\nThe original terminal inequality from Appendix A has the constants\n\n Psi(sqrt(eps))>=Gcost*eps,\n Gcost>=ell*mu*beta/c1=1/(k-k0),\n c1=beta*(k*ell*mu-b^2*w)>0.                          (18)\n\nAll remaining terms in Psi are nonnegative. The old terminal cut(1)\nensures its square root of the terminal row budget is defined.\nSince D<=0 and R>=0,\n\n                  eps=kR-D>=kR.\n\nThus\n\n R<=eps/k<=eps/(k-k0)<=Psi(sqrt(eps)),                (19)\n\nincluding eps=0 and R=0. This compares the ENTIRE previously derived\nterminal/full-quadratic-column formula; it does not construct its\nunderlying original terminal coefficients.\n\n## 6. Completion on the whole upper-terminal domain\n\nTo make conclusionII joint and self-contained, define\n\n zeta=b*mu/beta, kappa=zeta/H,\n xi=(H+1)/(H+gamma), theta0=1-xi^2,\n Yval=C^2*b^2*t^2, Zval=(v^2-1)^2.\n\nThe root equation for H gives\n\n H*(H+1)=zeta*(H+gamma)^2,\n xi^2=zeta+kappa, theta0+kappa=1-zeta.\n\nHere0<zeta<1, gamma>1,0<xi<1 and theta0>0. The strict bounds\nfollow from bt<1 and v>0. Direct expansion gives\n\n kappa*eps=theta0*D+(1-zeta)*A^2+zeta/a^2-Yval,\n\n (1-zeta)*eps=theta0*k*R+(1-zeta)*U+zeta*V\n                                            +(Zval-1)*Yval.   (20)\n\nFor example, kappa*k=b/(a^2*beta) and\n(1-bt)/beta=1-zeta yield the first identity; substitute\nD=kR-eps and U=A^2-Zval*Yval,V=1/a^2-Zval*Yval to get the second.\nOn (4), Zval>=1. Thus old R,Q,U imply\n\n eps>=U+zeta*V/(1-zeta)>=0,\n R<=(1-zeta)*eps/(theta0*k).                         (21)\n\nTo compare the calibration it suffices, by Section4, to prove\n\n           eps>=H/(1+H)*(sqrt(U)-sqrt(V))^2.          (22)\n\nIf a>=sqrt(2)-1, then U>=V>=0. The right side of (22) is at most\nU, so (21) proves it.\n\nIf D>=0, regardless of the first-step order, exact subtraction in\nthe first identity of (20) gives\n\n kappa*[eps-H/(1+H)*(sqrt(U)-sqrt(V))^2]\n   =theta0*D+(Zval-1)*Yval\n    +(1-zeta-zeta/(1+H))*U\n    +zeta*H*V/(1+H)+2*zeta*sqrt(U*V)/(1+H).           (23)\n\nThe coefficient of U equals theta0+kappa/(1+H)>0. Every displayed\nterm is nonnegative, proving (22) in this case as well.\n\nThe ONLY remaining case is a<sqrt(2)-1,D<0. It is exactly the\ndomain handled by (9)--(15), using the old F3>=0. These cases cover\nevery a>0,D. Hence the unmixed calibration family is redundant on\nALL of (4), with no low-first-step exception.\n\nFor the terminal condition note\n\n k0/k=kappa*gamma/(1-zeta),\n 0<1-zeta-kappa*gamma<theta0,\n 1/(k-k0)>(1-zeta)/(theta0*k).\n\nThe first strict inequality uses k>k0 from Appendix A; the second\nuses gamma>1. Combining(18),(21) proves the terminal condition.\n\nIf a<=sqrt(2)-1, the old F2>=0 still gives(16), without ANY\nrestriction on D. Combine this with the now proved(22) and use(13)\nand the unrestricted supremum to prove every mixture(2). This is\nthe complete lower-first-step statement of conclusionII.\n\nFor conclusionIII, Appendix A derives(5) from the same original\nfirst-row and lifted-cut identities under its stated product bounds.\nWhen delta>=0, solve the increasing inequality\nu*delta<=u*x^2+2*sqrt(Q)*x with x=sqrt(Erow/nu)>=0. Its nonnegative\nroot gives\n\n Erow>=nu*(sqrt(Qplus)-sqrt(Q))^2/u^2.\n\nAt delta=0, Erow>=0 gives the same result. Combining with(22),\n(13) and the same supremum proves every lambda mixture. The proof\nretains the FULL older bound(5), rather than its weaker strict scalar\ncorollary.\n\n## 7. What the lower-terminal comparison does and does not exclude\n\nIn fact, the portion of domain(3) with v<=sqrt(2) is already excluded\nat C>=193/25 by the OLD third cut alone. This prevents presenting\nthat comparison as a new threshold exclusion.\n\nTo prove the assertion let Acrit=1+sqrt(2)<169/70; the strict bound\nfollows from99^2>2*70^2. From a<=sqrt(2)-1\nwe have A<=Acrit, and D<=0 gives b(A+1+b)<=A. At b=61/100 the\nleft side minus the right side is\n\n 9821/10000-(39/100)*A\n     >9821/10000-(39/100)*(169/70)=2837/70000>0.\n\nThe function b(A+1+b) increases for b>0. Therefore b<61/100 and\n\n N=A+1+b<2817/700, Ecap:=1+v<=Acrit<1690/700.\n\nThe old F3>=0 gives Cc<=N*Ecap. Since c=C-N-Ecap, it implies\n\n                  C^2-(N+Ecap)*C-N*Ecap<=0.          (24)\n\nAt Cstar=193/25=5404/700, replacing N,Ecap by their upper bounds\nmakes the left side smaller, and its numerator on denominator700^2\nis\n\n 5404^2-(2817+1690)*5404-2817*1690=86658>0.\n\nThe bounded polynomial increases for C>=Cstar, since its derivative\nthere is at least(10808-4507)/700>0. Thus(24) is impossible at or\nabove threshold. No use of eps, of an equality locus, or of an assumed\nordering of the original steps entered this argument.\n\nThe genuinely unfinished lower-terminal comparison includes D>0.\nNo implication for that whole region is inferred from(9), whose\nnegative -(H+1)D term must then be retained.\n\n## 8. Exact diagnostic: the D hypothesis cannot simply be deleted\n\nThis diagnostic concerns the SMALL input list used in conclusionI,\nnot the complete older system. It audits a previously proposed\nbelow-threshold example and explicitly supplies its older C82 rejection.\n\nLet alpha be the unique positive root\n\n                         alpha^2*(11+2*alpha)=1.\n\nThe left side increases on positive arguments. Its value at1/3 is\n35/27>1; at47/160 it is2047743/2048000<1. Hence\n\n                    47/160<alpha<1/3<sqrt(2)-1.\n\nTake a=alpha,b=c=v=1. Then C=7+alpha<22/3<193/25 and\n\n R=(3/2)*(1-alpha^2*(11+2*alpha))=0,\n Q=1, U=(2+alpha)^2,\n D=12+4*alpha>0, eps=-D<0.\n\nThe two old cut energies are positive:\n\n 16*F2=15+50*alpha+15*alpha^2>0,\n 4*F3=15+18*alpha+3*alpha^2>0.\n\nThe old terminal cut also holds because v=1 and C>1. Therefore\nremoving D<=0 from the implication using just these listed inputs\nwould be false. This is not a threshold word or a complete-old-system\nsurvivor.\n\nHere is its explicit OLD triangle rejection. Let r82>1/alpha be\nthe positive solution of\n\n                     (r82+1)*(alpha*r82-1)=1/4.\n\nIts existence and uniqueness follow from strict increase of this\nquadratic for r>=1/alpha. Put z82=1/2 and\n\n K=conv{(r82,0),(-1,z82),(-1/2,0)},\n F_K(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.\n\nCompleting the square gives the unique maximizer p_x=P_K(x).\nAdding the two projection normal inequalities gives\n\n ||p_x-p_y||^2<=<p_x-p_y,x-y>, hence ||p_x-p_y||<=||x-y||.\n\nThe maximizing formula also gives\n\n 0<=F_K(x+d)-F_K(x)-<p_x,d>\n       <=<p_(x+d)-p_x,d><=||d||^2.\n\nThus F_K is differentiable with gradient p_x and is1-smooth.\nIt is convex as a maximum of affine functions, and0 is a minimizer:\nthe feasible vector g=0 gives F_K>=0, while F_K(0)=0.\nThe actual GD data are\n\n x0=((1+alpha)*r82-1,z82), g0=(r82,0),\n x1=(-1,z82),             g1=(-1,z82),\n x2=(1,-z82),             g2=(1,0),\n x3=(-1,-z82),            g3=(-1/2,0),\n x4=(-1/2,-z82),          g4=(-1/2,0).\n\nAll four updates have the stated word. At index0, pairing its normal\n(alpha*r82-1,z82) with the direction to(-1,z82) gives zero by the\nroot equation, and the direction to(-1/2,0) gives a negative product.\nAt index1 the normal is zero. At indices2 and4 the normal is vertical\ndownward: its products with horizontal base directions are zero and\nwith the upper vertex are negative. At index3 the normal is\n(-1/2,-z82); its product with the direction to the upper vertex is\n1/4-z82^2=0 and with the other base direction is negative. These\ncheck every vertex, hence every original ordered interpolation row.\n\nThe saturated values give primitive energy\n\n                ((1+alpha)*r82-1)^2-C^2/4.           (25)\n\nPut rbd=(9+alpha)/(2*(1+alpha)), so that\n(1+alpha)*rbd-1=C/2. Direct expansion and the defining equation\nfor alpha give\n\n (rbd+1)*(alpha*rbd-1)-1/4\n       =(29*alpha^2+138*alpha-43)/(8*(1+alpha)^2)>0.\n\nFor the strict sign, the numerator increases for positive alpha and\nat47/160 equals1021/25600>0. Also alpha*rbd-1>0:\nits numerator alpha^2+7*alpha-2 is already3649/25600>0 at47/160.\nThus rbd>1/alpha and strict monotonicity gives r82<rbd. Since\n(1+alpha)*r82-1>0, (25) is strictly negative. This is one member\nof the older C82 triangle family and rejects the diagnostic word.\nIt cannot be reused as an independence witness against the complete\nolder system.\n\n## 9. Remaining quantifiers and handoff\n\nNo actual h,Lambda,M with C>=193/25 has been constructed. The\nunmixed calibration comparison on v>=sqrt(2) is now complete for\nevery a>0, and every mixture is covered throughout0<a<=sqrt(2)-1.\nFor0<v<sqrt(2), conclusionI is a whole specified-domain comparison,\nbut D>0 remains outside it. Higher-first-step mixtures beyond the\nproduct hypotheses in conclusionIII also remain outside the proof.\n\nThe permitted boundaries bc=1,a=sqrt(2)-1,D=0,F2=0,F3=0,Q=0,\nQplus=0 and zero terminal row budget are retained. No argument divides\nby any of their slacks. Prices with an unattained supremum were handled\nby limits without asserting attainment. The domains require a,b,c,v>0;\nno zero-step conclusion or uniform margin as v tends to0 is inferred.\n\nFor an actual surviving word, ALL twenty original signs, exact flow,\nand PSD of the SAME full5x5 matrix still have to hold simultaneously.\nThe scalar comparisons neither choose those coefficients nor fix the\nremaining covariances. No restriction to one circulation fiber, rank,\nsupport pattern, reflection, or ordering has been declared exhaustive.\nIn particular, automatic validity of Psi does not match an original\nterminal row. No full-cone nonexistence conclusion follows from this\ncomparison.\n\nAccepted04219554e41d9750 still requires an actual length4 seed with\nsum h>=168/25, equivalently C>=193/25. Even a general no-seed theorem\nwould decide only that sufficient negative route. The original exact\nprofile target remains open. No fact_submit was authorized or called.\n\nThe initial direct attempt to use old middle/C82 tests to settle all\nof v<sqrt(2),D>0 did not establish either a dominance implication or\na complete-old-system survivor. The signed term in(20) and the\npositive D term requiring payment in(9) remain genuine gaps of that\nattempt. The completed comparison above is the result of retaining\nthe two OLD cut energies, rather than discarding them during scalar\noptimization. The auxiliary b>=1/2 coefficient detour was superseded\nby(9) and is not needed as a hypothesis.\n\n## Appendix A. Full original-array necessity arguments\n\nThe following original-row proof is reproduced in full from the\nworker's preceding draft and included as proof text to be checked\njointly. It is not a cited unverified premise. Its local numbering is\nindependent of Sections1--9 above. Its Hbar is the main text's H;\nits xi price is the main text's r; its rho is a separate local ratio.\nThe complete derivation retains every original coefficient and the\nfull five-gradient matrix. No excluded matching-locus analysis is\nused in this document.\n\n## 1. Original full interface and common identities\n\nLet\n\n    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,v),  a,b,c>0, v>=0, bc<=1,\n    u=1+a, beta=1+b, w=1+c,\n    A=2+a, N=3+a+b, Hpre=2+a+b, H0pre=1+a+b,\n    s=sum h, C=1+s=4+a+b+c+v,\n    t=c/(1+v), mu=1+t,\n    ell=a^2*b*(1-b*t), z=a*b*v*t,\n    R=ell*N^2+mu-C^2*a^2*b*beta*t^2,\n    D=N^2*b^2-A^2.\n\nLet e_i be the five original standard columns, indexed0 through4,\ne=sum e_i, and P_i=sum_(j<i)h_j. Define\n\n    T_ii=1, T_ij=h_j for j<i, T_ij=0 for j>i,\n    omega=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2s)^T,\n    Dmat=diag(h0(2-h0),h1(2-h1),h2(2-h2),h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)),\n    L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, M=Dmat+(LT+T^T L^T)/2.\n\nThe hypotheses are ALL twenty signs Lambda_ij>=0 for i!=j, zero\ndiagonal, L^T e=omega, and M>=0 on ALL of R^5. There is no support,\nrank, symmetry, reversal, or duplicated-gradient restriction.\n\nThe accepted identity is\n\n    V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=sum_(i,j)M_ij<g_i,g_j>,\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n    V_h=2sum_(i<4)h_i(f_i-f4)+sum_(i<4)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\n                                                 -s(s+1)||g4||^2, (1)\n\non formal data with x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i. Formal substitutions below\nare used as polynomial identities; no unproved interpolation is assumed.\n\nTe=e+P and the flow give e^TMe=0. Indeed e^TDmat e=-s^2-sum h_i^2\nand omega^T(e+P)=s^2+sum h_i^2. Applying PSD to e+r*y for both\nsigns of the real number r gives Me=0. The same argument proves\n\n    |<x,y>_M|^2<=||x||_M^2*||y||_M^2,\n    ||x||_M^2=0 implies Mx=0,                           (2)\n\nwhere <x,y>_M=x^TMy and ||x||_M^2=x^TMx. Under the flow, the\nindividual kernel equations are\n\n    sum_j Lambda_ij(P_j-P_i)=2h_i(1+P_i), i<4,\n    sum_(j<4)Lambda_4j(s-P_j)=2sC.                       (3)\n\nThese follow by expanding 2Me=2Dmat e+L(e+P)+T^T omega.\n\nPut\n\n    E=e0, J=e0+e1, B=e3+e4,\n    m=||E||_M^2, S=||J||_M^2, Tcut=||B||_M^2, rJB=<J,B>_M,\n    B0=Lambda30+Lambda40, B1=Lambda31+Lambda41,\n    Avec=u*E-a*beta*J-a*b*mu*B,\n    c03old=u*mu*z, c23old=a^2*b*beta*mu*v*t.\n\nThe three exact identities needed here are\n\n    R=ell*mu^2*Tcut+mu*||Avec||_M^2\n         +ell*mu*(Hpre*B0+beta*B1)+c03old*Lambda03+c23old*Lambda23, (4)\n\n    D=beta*((b*w*H0pre-u)*B0+b^2*w*B1-2*b*w*rJB-beta*S\n                     -u*Lambda20-b*v*c*(Lambda04+Lambda14)),       (5)\n\n    2<E,B>_M=a*B0-Lambda03-(1+v)*Lambda04.                         (6)\n\nTo prove (4), use saturated values f_i=x_i*g_i-g_i^2/2 on\n\n    x_base=(N,2+b,1,-c,-t), g_base=(1,1,1,-t,-t),\n    x_aux =(1,-a,ab,-abc,-abt), g_aux=(1,-a,ab,-abt,-abt).\n\nFor these values Q_ij=(g_i-g_j)(x_j-g_j). In ell times the base\nplus mu times the auxiliary, the complete nonzero residual list is\n\n    Q30=Q40=-ell*mu*Hpre, Q31=Q41=-ell*mu*beta,\n    Q03=-c03old, Q23=-c23old.\n\nEvery other ordered residual is zero. In particular Q13 cancels\nbecause ell=a^2*b*(1-bt); at Q03 use ell+ab(1+abt)=ab*u, and\nat Q23 use ell+a^2*b^2*mu=a^2*b*beta. Telescoping (1) gives\nR; the two gradient columns modulo e are -mu*B and Avec, proving (4).\n\nFor (5), start the scalar two-step cut at x0=A with gradients\n(1,1,-b), then extend FORMALLY with constant gradient -b and affine\nvalues through the last two steps. Its energy is -D and its column\nmodulo e is beta*J. Its negative residuals are\nQ20=Q30=Q40=-u*beta, and its positive residuals are\nQ03=Q13=w*b*beta, Q04=Q14=(w+v)*b*beta. All others vanish.\nThus (1) gives\n\n D=b*beta*(w*(Lambda03+Lambda13)+(w+v)*(Lambda04+Lambda14))\n                        -beta^2*S-u*beta*(Lambda20+B0).\n\nBesides (6), direct multiplication of T gives\n\n    2<e1,B>_M=beta*B0+b*B1-Lambda13-(1+v)*Lambda14.\n\nAdding these rows, and using (w+v)-w(1+v)=-vc, gives (5).\n\nThe additional ORIGINAL row used in this derivation is\n\n    2<E,J+B>_M=-a*Lambda20+Lambda02+w*Lambda03+w*Lambda04.           (7)\n\nIn fact e2=e-J-B and\n2M02=a*Lambda20-Lambda02-w*(Lambda03+Lambda04). This proves (7)\nwithout any PSD quotient or new support assumption.\n\n\n### Definition needed for the original positive root\n\nFor k>0 and real theta, set\n\n    F(k,theta)=b^2*w^2-z*theta\n          +(a^2+1/(k*mu))*theta^2/4-k*ell*mu^2.\n\nThis definition and the original row identities just proved suffice for\nthe following derivation. No capped optimizer or eps=0 matching is used.\n\n## 3. The unrestricted root and the new full-row remainder\n\nAssume v>0 for now. Then ell>0, z>0 and 1-bt>0. Set\n\n    k0=b^2*w/(ell*mu), kold=b^2*w^2/(ell*mu^2).\n\nThe minimum of F(k,theta) over all real theta is attained at\n\n    theta(k)=2*z*k*mu/(1+a^2*k*mu).\n\nIt is strictly decreasing as a function of k: at each fixed theta,\npartial F/partial k=-ell*mu^2-theta^2/(4*k^2*mu)<0, and the minimum\nis attained continuously. At k0 the minimum divided by mu^2 is\nb^2*(gamma-1)*(gamma+a^2*k0*mu)/(1+a^2*k0*mu)>0,\nwhere gamma=w/mu>1. At kold it is negative, since F(kold,0)=0 and\nthe negative linear term improves it for small positive theta.\nThere is therefore a unique root k in (k0,kold).\n\nHere is an explicit definition requiring no root search. Define\n\n    Hbar=(2*b*w-beta\n          +sqrt((beta-2*b*w)^2+4*b*w^2*(1-b*t)/mu))/(2*(1-b*t)),\n    k=Hbar/(a^2*mu), theta=2*z*k*mu/(1+Hbar),\n    x=b*w-a*theta/2=b*(w+Hbar*mu)/(1+Hbar), rho=x/beta,\n    hcoef=theta/(2*k*mu), jcoef=a^2*theta/2,\n    rstar=u*theta/2.\n\nThe positive Hbar solves\n\n    (1-bt)*Hbar^2+(beta-2*bw)*Hbar-b*w^2/mu=0,\n\nwhich follows by substituting theta(k) into F=0. Also\n\n    0<rho<1, hcoef+jcoef=z,\n    a*x-a*b*mu=hcoef, k*ell*mu^2+theta^2/(4*k*mu)\n                                            =2*b*w*x-x^2.         (8)\n\nThe inequalities use mu<w and bw<=beta, with strictness supplied\nby Hbar>0. The identities follow by expansion and F=0.\n\nFull original feasibility itself forces v<2. Indeed the terminal\ndiagonal is n=||e4||_M^2=sum_(j<4)Lambda4j-s(s+2)>=0.\nEvery weight in the last row of (3) is at least v. Hence\n\n    v*n<=s*(2C-(C+1)*v), so v<=2C/(C+1)<2.                       (9)\n\nWe now work on 0<v<2; v>=2 was already excluded by this original row.\nDefine the nonnegative correction\n\n    qcorr=max(0,((1+v)*rstar-beta*b*v*c)\n                         /(w+(1+v)*(1-rho))),\n    rshift=rstar-qcorr*(1-rho),\n    X=beta*J+x*B.\n\nThe EXACT original-array remainder is\n\n    eps:=k*R-D\n       =(1+Hbar)*||X||_M^2\n         -2*(k*mu*u*a+qcorr/beta)*<E,X>_M+k*mu*u^2*m\n         +cB*B0+c1*B1+c20*Lambda20+qcorr*Lambda02\n         +c03*Lambda03+c04*Lambda04+c14*Lambda14+c23*Lambda23,      (10)\n\nwhere\n\n    cB=k*ell*mu*Hpre-beta*(b*w*H0pre-u)+a*rshift,\n    c1=beta*(k*ell*mu-b^2*w), c20=beta*u-a*qcorr,\n    c03=k*c03old-rshift+w*qcorr,\n    c04=beta*b*v*c-(1+v)*rshift+w*qcorr,\n    c14=beta*b*v*c, c23=k*c23old.\n\nTo check the identity, first combine (4) and (5). Its Gram part is\n\n beta^2*||J||_M^2+2*beta*b*w*<J,B>_M+k*ell*mu^2*Tcut\n                                                 +k*mu*||Avec||_M^2.\n\nSubtract 2*rshift*<E,B>_M+2*qcorr*<E,J+B>_M from that Gram part.\nRestore them using (6) and (7); this gives precisely all costs in\n(10). Since rshift=rstar-qcorr*(1-rho), the subtraction differs\nfrom 2*rstar*<E,B> by 2*qcorr*<E,X>/beta. F=0 and (8) turn the\nuncorrected Gram into ||X||^2+k*mu*||uE-aX||^2. This proves (10).\n\nEvery displayed cost is nonnegative; cB,c1,c20,c03,c14,c23 are\nSTRICTLY positive. Here is the full sign and PSD check.\n\nLet\n\n    qmax=beta*u*(sqrt(k*mu*(1+Hbar))-a*k*mu).\n\nThen\n\n    0<=qcorr<qmax<beta*u/(2a).                                  (11)\n\nIf qcorr=0 this follows from Hbar>0. Otherwise put\ntau=sqrt(Hbar/(1+Hbar)) in (0,1), Vprod=vbc, and\nSden=2+b+c+v. Direct substitution gives\n\n    qcorr=(beta/a)*Vprod*(u*tau^2-a*beta)\n                              /(Sden-Vprod+Vprod*tau^2),\n    qmax=(beta*u/a)*tau/(1+tau).\n\nThe numerator of qcorr is positive in this case. The inequality\nqcorr<qmax is equivalent to\n\n    Vprod*(1+tau)*(u*tau-a*beta)<u*tau*Sden.\n\nIts left side is smaller than 2*Vprod*u*tau. Moreover\n2*Vprod<=2v<2+b+c+v=Sden, using bc<=1 and v<2. This proves the\nstrict inequality. The upper bound on qmax follows from\ntau/(1+tau)<1/2.\n\nAlso rshift>0: when qcorr>0 its numerator is less than\n(1+v)*rstar, while its denominator is strictly larger than\n(1+v)*(1-rho). Thus qcorr<rstar/(1-rho).\nAt k=k0 the part of cB before a*rshift equals u*(1-bc)>=0;\nit strictly increases with k. Hence cB>0. Since k>k0, c1>0.\nEquation (11) gives c20>beta*u/2>0. Finally\nrstar<k*u*mu*z=k*c03old and rshift<=rstar, proving c03>0.\nThe definition of qcorr gives c04>=0, and c14,c23>0.\n\nSet\n\n    alpha=(k*mu*u*a+qcorr/beta)/(1+Hbar), Y=X-alpha*E,\n    sigma=k*mu*u^2-(k*mu*u*a+qcorr/beta)^2/(1+Hbar)>0.\n\nThe positivity of sigma is exactly qcorr<qmax. Thus (10) is\n\n    eps=sigma*m+(1+Hbar)*||Y||_M^2+the nonnegative costs in (10).  (12)\n\nIn particular every full original certificate obeys D<=kR. This\nnecessity argument uses the actual original rows. Its comparison with\nthe complete older conditions is supplied by Sections1--9 of the\npresent joint text, not by a claim about an auxiliary optimizer.\n\n## 4. The actual quadratic column and joint first-row budget\n\nDefine\n\n    pquad=(1,-a,ab,-abc,abc*(v-1))^T,\n    Q=1-C^2*a^2*b^2*c^2*(v-1)^2,\n    delta=u^2-2, W0=1+bc*(v-1).\n\nTp=e and Le=0. Telescoping h_i(2-h_i)p_i^2=p_i^2-p_(i+1)^2 gives\n\n    ||pquad||_M^2=Q>=0,\n    2<E,pquad>_M=u*(m-delta)\n             +a^2*(beta*Lambda20+(1-bc)*Lambda30+W0*Lambda40).    (13)\n\nFor the row formula, let O0=sum_(j>0)Lambda0j and\nI0=Lambda20+Lambda30+Lambda40. Then m=1-a^2-aO0 and node0 flow\ngives Lambda10=O0+2u-I0. With Te0=u e-a e0 and\nMp=Dmat pquad+T^T L^T pquad/2 one obtains\n\n pquad^T L e0=uO0+2au-a*(beta*Lambda20+(1-bc)*Lambda30+W0*Lambda40),\n 2<E,pquad>_M=2(1-a^2)-a*pquad^T L e0,\n\nwhich gives (13). Both incoming weights 1-bc and W0 are nonnegative,\nand 1-bc<=W0, including v<1.\n\nBy (12), eps>=sigma*m+c20*Lambda20+cB*B0. Define\n\n    xi_cap=min(c20/(a^2*beta), cB/(a^2*W0)),\n\nomitting the second term if W0=0. For every real xi with\nxi<=xi_cap and xi<sigma/u,\n\n    eps>=xi*u*delta-xi^2*Q/(sigma-xi*u).                         (14)\n\nSubtract xi*u*delta using (13). All remaining multiplier coefficients\nare nonnegative: for xi>=0 this is the definition of xi_cap and the\norder of the incoming weights; for xi<0 the coefficients increase.\nThe remaining Gram expression after adding xi^2Q/(sigma-xi*u) is\n\n    ||sqrt(sigma-xi*u)*E+xi*pquad/sqrt(sigma-xi*u)||_M^2>=0.\n\nThus this is the same ORIGINAL matrix and column, not independent\nenergy selections. Let Qplus=Q+u^2*delta. Sending xi to -infinity\nshows Qplus>=0, and\n\n    Qplus=a^2*(A^2-C^2*b^2*c^2*(v-1)^2).\n\nThat prerequisite is the old scalar-return condition and is not\ncounted as an independent improvement.\n\nFor completeness, when Q,Qplus>0 the free maximizing xi is\nxi_free=(sigma/u)*(1-sqrt(Q/Qplus)). If xi_free<=xi_cap, (14)\nhas supremum\n\n    Bcal=(sigma/u^2)*(sqrt(Qplus)-sqrt(Q))^2.\n\nOtherwise its supremum is\n\n    Bcal=xi_cap*u*delta-xi_cap^2*Q/(sigma-xi_cap*u).\n\nThe latter case automatically has xi_cap<sigma/u. For Q>0,Qplus=0,\nthe supremum is sigma*Q/u^2. For Q=0,Qplus>=0 it is\nu*delta*min(xi_cap,sigma/u), and it is zero when Q=delta=0.\nThese statements follow on putting y=sigma-xi*u>0: the objective is\n\n sigma*delta+2*sigma*Q/u^2-(delta+Q/u^2)*y-Q*sigma^2/(u^2*y).\n\nThe earlier first-row budget is also retained, on its stated domain\n0<a<1 and a*eta<1, where\n\n    eta=b/(1+w+v), nu=(1+eta)*(1-a*eta)/a,\n    Erow=a+3+1/a-(u*delta/a)*eta-C^2*eta^2.\n\nIts exact identity is\n\n Erow=nu*(m+a*(Lambda20+B0))+(1+eta)^2*M11\n                 +(1+eta)*eta*((w+v)*Lambda12+v*Lambda13).        (15)\n\nHere is a direct derivation. For orthonormal physical vectors U,V set\ngfirst=-eta*U+sqrt(nu)*V and use saturated values on\n\n Xphys=(U+u*gfirst,U,-b*U,-(1+v)*eta*U,-eta*U),\n Gphys=(gfirst,U,-eta*U,-eta*U,-eta*U).\n\nThe nonzero residuals are Q20=Q30=Q40=-a*nu,\nQ12=-(1+eta)*(b-eta), and Q13=-(1+eta)*v*eta. Every other finite\nresidual is zero. Use ||gfirst||^2=[1+(1-a)*eta]/a and\n<U,gfirst>+a||gfirst||^2=1-a*eta to check the target0 residuals.\nThe energy telescope gives Erow and the two gradient columns modulo\ne are sqrt(nu)*E and (1+eta)*e1, proving (15).\n\nIn particular, for EVERY lambda>=0, (14) holds with its left side\neps+lambda*Erow, sigma replaced by sigma+lambda*nu, and cap replaced\nby\n\n min((c20+lambda*nu*a)/(a^2*beta),\n                         (cB+lambda*nu*a)/(a^2*W0)).              (16)\n\nThis follows by adding the TWO exact budgets before using (13), so\nthe same m and original incoming coefficients must pay for both.\nIt does not replace any of the older2948 conditions. For example,\nputting Wrow=max(beta,W0), Krow=max(u,a*Wrow) in (13),(15),(2) gives\n\n u*delta<=Krow*Erow/nu+2*sqrt(Q*Erow/nu).\n\nFor delta>0, v>1, ab<=1 and c(v-1)<=1, Krow=u and Q<1. These imply\nErow>nu*delta^2/u^2 and\n\n    b<(a+3)*(C-a-2)/((a+1)*(C+a+2)).\n\nTo see the latter equivalence, the direct factorization is\n\n u^2*(Erow-nu*delta^2/u^2)\n    =((a+3)-(delta+u*C)*eta)*((a+3)+(u*C-delta)*eta).\n\nThe second factor is positive since C>A and u*A>delta. Substituting\neta=b/(C-A-b) in the positive first factor gives the displayed\nbound. All its hypotheses remain.\n\n## 5. A further matched full-column inequality near equality\n\nIn this section v>0 and all constants are the ones already defined.\nLet\n\n    dphys=a*b*c*v=(1+v)*z,\n    nbar=s*(2C-(C+1)*v)/v>=0,\n    rcoef=ell*mu^2+mu*hcoef^2>0,\n    Lnorm=sqrt((u-a*alpha)^2/sigma+a^2/(1+Hbar)),\n    Lterm=sqrt((alpha-beta)^2/sigma+1/(1+Hbar)),\n    Kterm=(dphys/x)*Lterm*sqrt(nbar), Jfee=a/(2*x),\n    Pcost=max(ell*mu*Hpre/cB,ell*mu*beta/c1,c03old/c03,1/k),\n    Gcost=max(mu*Lnorm^2,Pcost).\n\nAll denominators are strictly positive. If nbar<0, the original\nterminal budget (9) already excludes the word; it is never square-rooted.\n\nEvery full original array satisfies the following explicit scalar test.\nWrite y=sqrt(eps), which requires eps>=0, and set\n\n tau_max(y)=(sqrt(Q)+Lnorm*y\n       +sqrt((sqrt(Q)+Lnorm*y)^2+4*jcoef*(Kterm*y+Jfee*y^2)))\n                                                            /(2*jcoef),\n Psi(y)=rcoef*tau_max(y)^2\n            +2*mu*hcoef*Lnorm*tau_max(y)*y+Gcost*y^2.\n\nThen\n\n                              R<=Psi(sqrt(eps)).                 (17)\n\nHere is the full derivation retaining the actual quadratic column.\nThe vector identity, checked in all five coordinates, is\n\n    pquad=uE-aX-jcoef*B+dphys*e4+ab*e.\n\nThe original terminal rows give\n\n    2<E,e4>_M=a*Lambda40-Lambda04,\n    2<J,e4>_M=2<E,e4>_M+beta*Lambda40+b*Lambda41-Lambda14.\n\nSince X=Y+alpha E, they imply\n\n x<B,e4>_M=(alpha-beta)<E,e4>_M+<Y,e4>_M\n                              -beta*(beta*Lambda40+b*Lambda41-Lambda14)/2.\n\nTaking the inner product of pquad with B therefore gives\n\n <B,pquad>_M=(u-a*alpha)<E,B>_M-a<Y,B>_M-jcoef*Tcut\n       +(dphys/x)*((alpha-beta)<E,e4>_M+<Y,e4>_M)\n       -dphys*beta*(beta*Lambda40+b*Lambda41-Lambda14)/(2*x).       (18)\n\nLet eps_G=sigma*m+(1+Hbar)||Y||_M^2 and let eps_C be the cost sum\nin (12). Thus eps=eps_G+eps_C with both nonnegative. Weighted\nCauchy--Schwarz gives\n\n ||(u-a*alpha)E-aY||_M<=Lnorm*sqrt(eps_G),\n ||(alpha-beta)E+Y||_M<=Lterm*sqrt(eps_G).\n\nAlso ||e4||_M^2<=nbar by (9), and\nc14=beta*dphys/a. Drop the negative Lambda40,Lambda41 terms in\n(18), use Lambda14<=eps_C/c14, and use\n<B,pquad>_M>=-sqrt(Tcut*Q). The result is\n\n jcoef*Tcut<=sqrt(Tcut*Q)\n        +(Lnorm*sqrt(Tcut)+Kterm)*sqrt(eps_G)+Jfee*eps_C.          (19)\n\nFor the other direction (4) and Avec=(u-a*alpha)E-aY+hcoef*B give\n\n R<=rcoef*Tcut+2*mu*hcoef*Lnorm*sqrt(Tcut*eps_G)\n                                    +mu*Lnorm^2*eps_G+Pcost*eps_C\n  <=rcoef*Tcut+2*mu*hcoef*Lnorm*sqrt(Tcut*eps)+Gcost*eps.           (20)\n\nThe cost bound follows by comparing each cost in (4) to the cost of\nthe SAME multiplier in (12); their maximum ratio is precisely Pcost.\nThus costs and energies have not been chosen from different arrays.\nReplacing eps_G,eps_C separately by eps in (19) gives, for\ntcut=sqrt(Tcut)>=0,\n\n jcoef*tcut^2-(sqrt(Q)+Lnorm*y)*tcut-(Kterm*y+Jfee*y^2)<=0.\n\nIts nonnegative root is tau_max(y). The right side of (20) increases\nwith tcut>=0, which proves (17).\n\nThis supplies a quantified positive-gap exclusion test. Psi is\ncontinuous and strictly increasing on [0,infinity), tends to infinity,\nand\n\n    Psi(0)=rcoef*Q/jcoef^2.\n\nConsequently, if R>rcoef*Q/jcoef^2, there is a unique y0>0 with\nPsi(y0)=R, and every full array must have\n\n                           eps>=y0^2>0.                         (21)\n\nStrict increase follows from the explicit nondecreasing positive\ncoefficients in tau_max and Gcost>0. This is an algebraic definition\nand proof; no numerical root finding is used. In particular Q=0,R>0\nforces a positive eps, even when delta=0 and the first-row bound\n(14) alone is zero. {"id": "39115c0842ba32a7", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T15:10:09.378720+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "C100 COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED dense-family obstruction with STRONG long projections: on original enclosing-m H_m, assume nonpositive adjacent queried correlations and kval_i=(f_i+q_i/2)/q_i in[1+a/2,1+a]. All initial anchors, support ratios, optimum slacks and the actual terminal are otherwise free. The original remote reverse row gives V<50 beta^(2n)c^(-2m)(243/256)^(2^m/8) exp(E_mix) for m>=256. E_mix explicitly prices kval variation and negative distance-two departures from the Markov identity; E_mix<=26 TV_val+D_dep. Thus the ENTIRE constant-kval dense Markov class, and the stated variable/non-Markov classes with E_mix=o(n), have B_n V->0 and vanish against accepted SAME-word Huber, K, ray and augmented lens witnesses. No cap t_i<=1 is imposed. Actual H6 constant-ratio zero-defect instances lie below1/256<C59. Full anchor norm, true-radius control and a legal dense h=(4,7/5) example with t0=2 and payoff112/1825 are included; changing its parameter to r=1/2 passes local rows but violates one remote row. C100 and C99 have crossing scopes; low-reserve strong-projection laws remain open. Original Goal OPEN.", "evidence": "# C100: remote reserve transport prices strong dense projections\n\nStatus: COMPLETE JOINT UNVERIFIED DRAFT. Actual runtime round2 of the\nallocation launched under guidance4226374d0649bf55; completed baseline29,\ncompleted ceiling31, attempted ceiling32 including the historical\ninterruption. No numerical work or formal verification is authorized.\nThe original arbitrary-word exact-profile Goal, with W=2R, remains OPEN.\n\nAccepted inputs are1252b8823bb5a290 for the original directed rows and\nthe static conjugate interpolant, and b98bb807d514e80d for the original\nbenchmark and scalar witnesses. Accepted1fca3e5dd555cb4a,\ndfde2fa326c908bf and e9a55f57461f253c are used only for the stated\nsame-word comparisons. C98 and C99 are unverified context, not premises.\nAll new mathematical arguments used here are proved together below.\n\n## 1. Actual functions, the enlarged dependence class, and the result\n\nLet F be an everywhere finite convex differentiable function on a finite\nEuclidean space, with1-Lipschitz gradient and a nonempty minimizer set.\nFor a supplied word h of length n consider its actual trajectory\n\n    X_(i+1)=X_i-h_i g_i,  g_i=grad F(X_i),\n    f_i=F(X_i)-min F,  q_i=||g_i||^2,\n    R_init=dist(X_0,argmin F)>0,  V=2f_n/R_init^2.\n\nAll directed interpolation inequalities concern this ONE function.\nThe actual terminal gradient is unrestricted. If any queried gradient\nvanishes then all later iterates minimize F and V=0. In the remaining\ncase define, at the queried indices,\n\n    delta_i=f_i-q_i/2,  Jval_i=f_i+q_i/2,\n    kval_i=Jval_i/q_i=1+delta_i/q_i.\n\nFor an included minimizer chosen as origin define separately\n\n    eta_i=<X_i,g_i>/q_i,\n    z_i=<X_i,g_i>-f_i-q_i/2.\n\nThe optimum rows give delta_i>=0 and z_i>=0, and\n\n    kval_i=eta_i-z_i/q_i.                         (1)\n\nThus a restriction on kval is a restriction on actual values, not on\nsupport about an arbitrarily named minimizer. No restriction on eta_i,\nz_i, anchor density, signs of initial coordinates, or dimension is made.\n\nUse the ORIGINAL enclosing-m words, with parameters fixed throughout\nevery recursive child:\n\n    m>=4, N=2^m, n=N-1, sigma=1+sqrt(2),\n    beta=1-1/m, c=sigma(1+1/m), a=beta/c,\n    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.\n\nHere h=H_m, 0<a<sqrt(2)-1, and the shortest step is lshort=1+a.\nThe following value range is the substantive restriction of this draft:\n\n    1+a/2 <= kval_i <= 1+a  for every queried i.  (2)\n\nAssume all adjacent QUERIED gradients have nonpositive inner product.\nSet, for0<=i<n-1,\n\n    d_i=h_i-1,\n    t_i=-<g_i,g_(i+1)>/q_i >=0.\n\nThere is NO cap t_i<=1. For each odd i=1,3,...,n-4 define the signed\ndeparture from the distance-two Markov identity\n\n    defect2_i=<g_i,g_(i+2)>/q_i-t_i t_(i+1).       (3)\n\nThe middle physical step in each such pair is h_(i+1)=lshort.\nDefine for k in[1+a/2,1+a], writing e=k-1,\n\n    Ushort_a(k)=[a-(1-a)e]/[1+(1-a)e],\n    Aenv_a(k)=e[k+lshort Ushort_a(k)]/k^2,\n    Cenv_a(k)=Ushort_a(k)(lshort-k)/k^2,\n    Epair_a(k,d)=Aenv_a(k)+Cenv_a(k)d^2.            (4)\n\nThese are explicit real scalar functions; Epair is strictly positive.\nFor the indicated odd indices put\n\n    eleft_i=|kval_i-kval_(i+1)|,\n    eright_i=|kval_(i+2)-kval_(i+1)|,\n    D_dep=sum_i d_i max(0,-defect2_i)\n                    /[kval_(i+2) Epair_a(kval_(i+1),d_i)],\n    E_mix=sum_i [(2/a)eleft_i+(1+4/a^2)eright_i]+D_dep,\n    TV_val=sum_(i=0)^(n-2)|kval_(i+1)-kval_i|.\n\nAll sums over odd indices above stop at n-4. These quantities are paid\ndefects of an ACTUAL trajectory; they are neither anchor counts nor a\nclaim of interpolation sufficiency. For m>=256,\n\n    E_mix <=26 TV_val+D_dep.                       (5)\n\nThe principal bound proved below is, for m>=256,\n\n    V <= [64(1+2a)/(45a^3 beta^2)]\n          beta^(2n)c^(-2m)(243/256)^(N/8)exp(E_mix)\n      < 50 beta^(2n)c^(-2m)(243/256)^(N/8)exp(E_mix). (6)\n\nIn particular, the entire class (2) with E_mix=o(n) has B_n V->0.\nThis includes every constant-kval, arbitrarily dense Markov anchor,\nwith arbitrary nonstationary nonpositive correlations and arbitrarily\nstrong permitted long projections. It also includes variable-kval\nMarkov families with TV_val=o(n). More generally exact Markov dependence\nis unnecessary: E_mix=o(n) is sufficient for the same obstruction.\n\nNo upper bound on unrestricted W_n(H_m) is asserted. Leaving the value\nrange(2), permitting positive adjacent correlations, paying linear\nvalue variation, or paying the indicated negative distance-two defects\nare genuinely outside this conclusion. No necessary budget is described\nas a sufficient construction.\n\n## 2. Original rows and exact remote slack transport\n\nThe accepted original residual is\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,X_j-X_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2<=0. (7)\n\nFor a general step h_i=1+d_i>1, adding both adjacent rows gives\n\n    q_(i+1)/q_i <=d_i+(d_i-1)t_i.\n\nCauchy--Schwarz gives t_i^2<=q_(i+1)/q_i, so\n\n    (t_i+1)(t_i-d_i)<=0,  0<=t_i<=d_i.             (8)\n\nThe nonnegative lower bound uses the assumed adjacent sign. In\nparticular at a shortest step q_(i+1)<=a q_i.\n\nThe reverse adjacent slack, a different quantity from z_i, is\n\n    ell_rev_i=-Q_(i+1,i)\n       =delta_i-delta_(i+1)+d_i t_i q_i-q_(i+1)>=0. (9)\n\nSuppose for this paragraph the queried unit directions g_i/sqrt(q_i)\nhave the FULL Markov product law: their inner product at indices r<s\nis the product of the adjacent normalized inner products from r to s.\nSet p_(r,s)=product_(k=r)^(s-1)(-t_k), with p_(s,s)=1.\nThe amplitude ratios telescope to give <g_r,g_s>=q_r p_(r,s).\nFor i+1<j<n, expanding(7) and the GD recursion\ngives the exact transport identity\n\n    Q_(j,i)-Q_(j,i+1)\n      =(delta_(i+1)-delta_i)(1-p_(i+1,j))\n                         -ell_rev_i p_(i+1,j).      (10)\n\nIndeed the difference before substituting(9) is\n\n    delta_(i+1)-delta_i\n         +p_(i+1,j)[q_(i+1)-d_i t_i q_i],\n\nwhich equals(10). In particular the first remote reverse row is\n\n    Q_(i+2,i)=-ell_rev_(i+1)\n       +(1+t_(i+1))(delta_(i+1)-delta_i)\n       +t_(i+1)ell_rev_i.                          (11)\n\nThus adjacent reverse equalities at i and i+1 force\ndelta_(i+1)<=delta_i. With a constant kval>1, they force\nq_(i+1)<=q_i; hence t_i>1 is then impossible by Cauchy--Schwarz.\nAttempting to saturate adjacent rows while using a strong projection\nomits exactly the reserve increase charged by(11).\n\nEquation(11) alone is not assumed sufficient for legality. The\nwhole-family bound below concerns actual functions satisfying ALL rows.\nFor the larger non-Markov class we use(3) explicitly instead of\ntransferring(10) outside its hypothesis.\n\n## 3. A short-step cap that retains the actual value ratios\n\nConsider a shortest step from a queried point of ratio k to another\nqueried point of ratio k', both in(2), and let its projection be u>=0.\nWrite r=q_next/q_current. The forward adjacent row is\n\n    (k'-1)r >= (k-1)-a+u.                          (12)\n\nIts sum with the reverse row yields r<=a-(1-a)u by(8).\nCombining these inequalities, without replacing either value, gives\n\n    u <= Ushort_a(k')+(k'-k)/[1+(1-a)(k'-1)].       (13)\n\nThe numerator of Ushort is positive on the whole range(2). Its derivative\nin k equals -(1-a)(1+a)/[1+(1-a)(k-1)]^2, of absolute value at most1.\nConsequently\n\n    u<=Ushort_a(k)+2|k'-k|.                        (14)\n\nAlso Ushort_a(k)>=a^2/(1+a-a^2)>=a^2/2. The first inequality uses its\nmonotonic decrease in k, and the second uses1+a-a^2<2.\n\n## 4. Exact two-step envelope, including a changed dependence law\n\nTake an odd index i in the range used in Section1. Abbreviate its three\nratios by k0,k1,k2; put s=q_(i+1)/q_i, r=q_(i+2)/q_i,\nt=t_i, u=t_(i+1), d=d_i, and v=defect2_i.\nThe reverse adjacent row at i gives\n\n    k1 s <= k0-1+d t.                              (15)\n\nThe ORIGINAL remote reverse row from i+2 to i, expanded directly, is\n\n    Q_(i+2,i)/q_i=k2 r-(k0-1)+d(tu+v)-lshort u s.\n\nThus, writing vminus=max(0,-v),\n\n    k2 r <=(k0-1)(1+lshort u/k1)\n              +u(lshort-k1)d t/k1+d vminus.        (16)\n\nThe coefficient lshort-k1 is nonnegative by(2). Since0<=t<=d,\nreplace dt by d^2 on the right. In the constant-ratio, v=0 case,\n(13) allows u<=Ushort_a(k1);(16) then proves exactly\n\n    r<=Epair_a(k1,d).                              (17)\n\nNo limitation on the size of t below its universal bound d was used.\n\nFor variable ratios put eleft=|k0-k1| and eright=|k2-k1|.\nAll coefficients in the right side of(16) after replacing dt are\nnonnegative. Therefore its vminus-free part is bounded by Epair times\n\n    (k1/k2) max(1,(k0-1)/(k1-1))\n                          max(1,u/Ushort_a(k1)).\n\nBecause k1-1>=a/2, (14) and the lower bound following it give\n\n    k1/k2 <=exp(eright),\n    max(1,(k0-1)/(k1-1))<=exp(2eleft/a),\n    max(1,u/Ushort_a(k1))<=exp(4eright/a^2).\n\nIf E0=(2/a)eleft+(1+4/a^2)eright, we have\n\n    r<=Epair exp(E0)+d vminus/k2\n      <=Epair exp(E0+d vminus/(k2 Epair)).          (18)\n\nFor the second inequality factor Epair exp(E0), use E0>=0 and\n1+x<=exp(x) for x>=0. This proves the asserted extension to non-Markov\nlaws with an explicit signed price. Positive v only strengthens the\nremote row and needs no penalty. The terminal gradient is not used.\n\n## 5. Uniform real envelope coefficients\n\nFor0<a<=sqrt(2)-1 and k in(2),\n\n    0<Aenv_a(k)<=1/3,   0<=Cenv_a(k)<=a^2/4.        (19)\n\nHere is a direct proof. Set e=k-1 in[a/2,a], D=1+(1-a)e and\nU=Ushort_a(k). It is positive and decreasing in e. Hence\n\n    U <= a(1+a)/(2+a-a^2) <=a(1+a)/2.\n\nAlso a-e<=a/2 and k>=1+a/2, so\n\n    Cenv=U(a-e)/k^2\n       <=a^2(1+a)/[4(1+a/2)^2]<=a^2/4.\n\nFor the bound on Aenv, consider A(e)=e[k+(1+a)U]/k^2 on0<=e<=a.\nDirect differentiation gives\n\n    A'(e)={k+(1+a)(1-e)U\n                -e k(1-a)(1+a)^2/D^2}/k^3.\n\nThe middle term is nonnegative. Moreover\ne(1-a)(1+a)^2/D^2<=a(1+a)<1, since a<=sqrt(2)-1.\nThus A is increasing. At e=a, using U=a^2/(1+a-a^2),\n\n    A(a)=a/(1+a-a^2)<=1/3,\n\nthe last inequality being equivalent to a^2+2a<=1. This proves(19).\nIn particular\n\n    Epair_a(k,d)<=1/3+(a^2/4)d^2.                  (20)\n\nThis envelope holds at every macro separately, even when its k changes.\n\n## 6. The complete norm and the actual closest distance\n\nFor the full product-correlated subclass, write A_i=sqrt(q_i) and\nu_i=g_i/A_i, for0<=i<n. Its correlations are real numbers rho_i in[-1,0]\nwith <u_i,u_j>=product_(k=i)^(j-1)rho_k. Actual real coordinates are\n\n    u_0=e_0,\n    u_(i+1)=rho_i u_i+sqrt(1-rho_i^2)e_(i+1),       (21)\n\nwhere each nondegenerate innovation is orthogonal to the past. The\nliteral finite sign law is\n\n    P(s_0,...,s_(n-1))=2^(-n)\n                     product_(i<n-1)(1+rho_i s_i s_(i+1)),\n    s_i in{-1,1}.                                 (22)\n\nSumming the last sign successively proves normalization. Equivalently,\nstart with a fair sign and independent bond signs of means rho_i; this\ngives the stated products. Interior rho give positive probability to\nevery sign word. This law is not a substitute for interpolation.\n\nConversely the product identities imply that\n(u_(i+1)-rho_i u_i)/sqrt(1-rho_i^2) is a unit vector orthogonal to all\nearlier u_j, by expanding its inner products. Thus(21) also describes\nthe canonical coordinates of any such product law.\n\nLet0 be any included minimizer and let X_perp be the component of X_0\nperpendicular to every queried gradient. Since previous gradients are\northogonal to each new innovation, its initial coefficient\nw_(i+1)=<X_0,e_(i+1)> satisfies\n\n    eta_(i+1)A_(i+1)=rho_i(eta_i-h_i)A_i\n                            +sqrt(1-rho_i^2)w_(i+1).\n\nThe initial e_0 coefficient is eta_0 A_0. Orthogonal decomposition\ntherefore gives the COMPLETE identity\n\n    ||X_0||^2=eta_0^2 q_0+||X_perp||^2\n      +sum_(i:|rho_i|<1)\n         [eta_(i+1)A_(i+1)-rho_i(eta_i-h_i)A_i]^2\n                                             /(1-rho_i^2). (23)\n\nAt rho_i=-1 the numerator must vanish and there is no new summand.\nEvery dense initial coefficient and any perpendicular reserve is paid.\nThis is a norm about the included minimizer, not automatically the true\nclosest-minimizer distance. For the whole-class proof we instead use\n\n    q_0<=R_init^2,                                 (24)\n\nwhich follows from gradient Lipschitzness between X_0 and EVERY minimizer,\nfollowed by taking the infimum. Hence the proof neither drops any initial\ncost nor incorrectly declares a conveniently chosen minimizer closest.\nThe explicit finite interpolants in Section9 have unique minimizer0 and\ntheir true distances are computed from all their coordinates.\n\n## 7. Original enclosing-m product and the unrestricted final two steps\n\nAt level j, write dlev_j=beta c^(j-1) for the actual offset; that level occurs\nN/2^(j+1) times. Level0 is a. Multiplying the recursive words gives\n\n    D_word=product_(i<n)(h_i-1)\n          =a^(N-1)c^(N-m-1)=beta^n c^(-m).         (25)\n\nFor example its a and c exponents satisfy the recursions obtained by\nsquaring the child product and multiplying by a c^j; induction gives\n2^j-1 and2^j-j-1 for H_j, proving(25).\n\nUse the first physical shortest step only through q_1<=a q_0 from(8).\nApply(18) to each odd index1,3,...,n-4. These pairs include every bridge\nof level at least1 except the FINAL level1 bridge. Consequently\n\n    q_(n-2)/q_0 <= a exp(E_mix)\n       product_(j=1)^(m-1) Eupper(dlev_j)^(b_j),    (26)\n    Eupper(d)=1/3+(a^2/4)d^2,\n    b_1=N/4-1,  b_j=N/2^(j+1) for j>=2.\n\nThe last two physical steps are exactly1+beta and1+a, both below2.\nIntegration of the1-Lipschitz gradient along a step yields\n\n    f_(i+1)<=f_i-h_i(2-h_i)q_i/2.\n\nTherefore f_n<=f_(n-2), with no condition on g_n, no condition on its\nsupport or value ratio, and no Markov extension to it. From(2),(24),\n\n    V <=(1+2a)q_(n-2)/R_init^2\n      <=(1+2a)a exp(E_mix) product_j Eupper(dlev_j)^b_j. (27)\n\nThis finite formula is valid already for every m>=4.\n\nFor m>=256 we now bound its product explicitly. The elementary estimates\n\n    a^2 beta^2>1/6,  beta^4>49/50,  c^2>23/4       (28)\n\nfollow as follows. The ratio beta^2/(1+1/m) is increasing with m, and at\nm=256 it exceeds79/80, since80*65025>79*65792. Also\nsigma<29/12, so a beta>237/580 and6*237^2>580^2. This proves the first\ninequality. Bernoulli's inequality gives beta^4>=1-4/m>=63/64>49/50.\nFinally sigma^2=3+2sqrt(2)>23/4 proves the last inequality.\n\nNormalize each Eupper by a^2 dlev_j^2. At level1, (28) gives\n\n    Eupper(dlev_1)/(a^2dlev_1^2)<9/4.\n\nAt level2, a^2dlev_2^2=beta^4, so the ratio is at most\n50/147+1/4<3/5. At every level j>=3, a^2dlev_j^2>=beta^4 c^2, so the ratio\nis at most200/3381+1/4<5/16.\nThere are N/4-1, N/8, and N/8-1 such factors respectively. Thus\n\n    product_j [Eupper(dlev_j)/(a^2dlev_j^2)]^b_j\n      <(64/45)(243/256)^(N/8).                     (29)\n\nIndeed (9/4)^2(3/5)(5/16)=243/256, and the two missing factors contribute\n(4/9)(16/5)=64/45. The unnormalized baseline product is\n\n    product_j(a^2dlev_j^2)^b_j=D_word^2/(a^4 beta^2). (30)\n\nIt consists of every step offset squared except the first shortest\nstep and the last pair of offsets(beta,a). Multiplying(29),(30) into\n(27), then using(25), proves the first line of(6).\n\nFor m>=256, (28) gives a>2/5, while beta^2>49/50 and1+2a<2. Thus\n64(1+2a)/(45a^3 beta^2)<50, proving the simpler second line. The same\na bound gives2/a<=5 and1+4/a^2<=26. The edges counted in the eleft and\neright sums are disjoint adjacent edges, proving(5).\n\n## 8. Original-B and every applicable same-word comparison\n\nAccepted b98bb807d514e80d uses the ORIGINAL all-split benchmark B_n,\nnot a chosen recursion tree, and gives B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3)<=1+4*3^m.\nConsequently (6) implies\n\n    B_n V <50(1+4*3^m) beta^(2n)c^(-2m)\n                        (243/256)^(N/8)exp(E_mix). (31)\n\nFor E_mix=o(n) the right side tends to zero. More quantitatively, any\nfamily satisfying the stated assumptions with liminf B_n V>0 must obey\n\n    liminf E_mix/N >=(1/8)log(256/243).             (32)\n\nTo check(32), choose a positive eventual lower bound v0 for B_n V and\ntake logarithms in(6). The exact necessary inequality is\n\n    E_mix >=(N/8)log(256/243)+2n log(1/beta)+2m log c\n          -log B_n-log[64(1+2a)/(45a^3 beta^2)]+log v0.\n\nAfter division by N the extra terms tend to zero. In particular a\nMarkov family in(2) needs linear total variation of kval unless it leaves\nanother hypothesis: from(5), liminf TV_val/N is at least\nlog(256/243)/208 when D_dep=0 and the normalized payoff stays positive.\nThis is a necessary condition only.\n\nThere is a direct SAME-WORD comparison. If S_m=sum H_m and M_m=1+2S_m,\nthe recurrence S_(j+1)=2S_j+1+a c^j gives\n\n    S_m=N-1+a(c^m-N)/(c-2),   M_m<2c^m.            (33)\n\nFor the inequality, c>sigma>2, c^m>N, and\na/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)]<1 since sigma(sigma-2)=1. Thus S_m<c^m-1,\nwhich proves the displayed strict bound on M_m.\n\nThe global Huber witness1/M_m is contained in K_n(H_m) by accepted\nb98bb807d514e80d, in F_ray_n(H_m) by accepted1fca3e5dd555cb4a, and in\nV_lens(H_m) by accepted dfde2fa326c908bf. Therefore\n\n    V/(1/M_m)<100 beta^(2n)c^(-m)(243/256)^(N/8)exp(E_mix),\n    V/K_n(H_m), V/F_ray_n(H_m), V/V_lens(H_m)\n                                         <=V/(1/M_m). (34)\n\nAll these ratios vanish for E_mix=o(n). No claim about a particular lens\nparameter or a Chebyshev word is transferred to H_m.\n\nFor the ACTUAL m=6 word, a<3/10 and c^2<8 follow from\n7/5<sqrt(2)<10/7. Since beta=5/6, (20) implies\n\n    Eupper(dlev_j)<1/3+8^(j-1)/64.\n\nFor j=1,2,3,4,5 these are respectively bounded above by\n3/8,1/2,4/3,9,65. In the constant-kval, zero-defect case E_mix=0,\n(27) and its exact counts15,8,4,2,1 give\n\n    V <(1/2)(3/8)^15(1/2)^8(4/3)^4 9^2 65 <1/256.\n\nThe second inequality uses3/8<1/2, (4/3)^4<4, 9^2<128 and65<128.\nHence this entire class on the ACTUAL H6 is below\n1/256<1/50<56/2711, the accepted C59 witness e9a55f57461f253c.\nNo C59 assertion is transferred to later enclosing-m subwords.\n\nThe accepted orthogonal-moment obstruction is consistent with the\northogonal subfamily here; it is not used as an unproved extension to\nnonorthogonal gradients. The other accepted restricted obstructions and\nconditional seed theorem supply no additional pointwise lower witness\nfor the present actual words, so no further comparison is inferred.\n\n## 9. Real dense data: strong projection, all rows, exact radius\n\nThe excluded class is not an implicit t<=1 class. Here is a complete\nthree-dimensional family, useful also for testing the remote row.\nTake h=(4,7/5), a=2/5, kval=6/5, eta=5/4, and a real parameter r>0.\nIn the standard orthonormal coordinates e_0,e_1,e_2 put\n\n    g_0=e_0, g_1=-2e_0+e_1, g_2=sqrt(r)e_2,\n    X_0=(5/4)e_0+(3/4)e_1+(5/4)sqrt(r)e_2,\n    X_1=X_0-4g_0, X_2=X_1-(7/5)g_1,\n    q_0=1, q_1=5, q_2=r, f_i=(7/10)q_i.\n\nThe support eta is5/4 at all three points; delta_i=q_i/5 and\nz_i=q_i/20 are strictly positive. The queried projection t_0=2 exceeds1.\nThe full normalized directions have rho_0=-2/sqrt(5), rho_1=0;\ntheir distance-two product is exact. The sign law is the positive law\n\n    P(s_0,s_1,s_2)=(1/8)[1-(2/sqrt(5))s_0s_1].\n\nThe initial coefficients are all nonzero. Every original non-optimum\ndirected residual is as follows:\n\n    Q_01=-9/5,             Q_10=-1/5,\n    Q_12=-1-r/5,           Q_21=(6r-5)/5,\n    Q_02=-r/5,             Q_20=(6r-1)/5.           (35)\n\nThe two optimum orientations are Q_(star,i)=-q_i/5 and\nQ_(i,star)=-q_i/20. Hence the full data are legal exactly when0<r<=1/6;\nthey pass every adjacent and optimum row for0<r<=5/6. At r=1/2 only\nthe remote reverse Q_20=2/5 is violated. This is an explicit counterexample\nto replacing full legality by these local rows, even with a positive\njoint law, dense initial coordinates and positive reserves and slacks.\n\nFor0<r<=1/6 define the actual function from the accepted construction:\n\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+\n          max{0, max_(i=0,1,2)(<X_i-g_i,v>-delta_i)},\n    F(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H(v)). {"id": "ec1fba9a30a3fa47", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T15:17:21.615403+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "proof_attempt", "claim": "max37 COMPLETE JOINT DRAFT, UNVERIFIED: exact minimum true-radius two-predecessor rule for a compact gradient-set conjugate with an initial point equal to its gradient and a radial affine suffix, all zero/unit/large-step cases and fixed absolute suffix jets included. Original arbitrary-word/profile goal remains OPEN.", "evidence": "# Max37: exact triangular predecessors of a fixed affine suffix\n\nStatus: COMPLETE JOINT PROOF DRAFT, UNVERIFIED.
